| N59 | FR-RT-01, FR-RT-02, ADR-005, and item 52 said that `GET /api/v1/ws` upgrades to a WebSocket, authenticated by the session cookie or a token, over which a client subscribes to the projects that it can read, without learning whether the others exist, resumes with `since` or is told `resync_required`, and hears heartbeats, and that one `LISTEN` connection per process hears notifications of `(project, seq)` alone, after which the hub fetches the operations from the log, but not what the socket's messages are, when `resync_required` is sent, what a refusal looks like, whether a subscriber's credential and access are checked again once it subscribes, what bounds a socket and a user's sockets, how an operation whose notification was lost reaches its subscribers, which token scope a socket needs, or how a page of another site is kept from a browser's session, which the browser sends with a WebSocket whatever its origin. | Decided, with PRD 1.45. **Hub:** package `server/internal/realtime`, one `Hub` per process, whose sockets `coder/websocket` (PRD §10.8) upgrades. Its `LISTEN` connection, on `storycreep_operations` (`realtime.Channel`), is one of the application login that the pool no longer holds (`database.DB.Dedicated`), which the first subscription opens, and which it opens again, after a second, doubling to 30 seconds, whenever it fails. The pipeline's `Notifier` (`realtime.Notifier`) sends each batch's notification once the batch has committed, with `pg_notify` outside a transaction (`database.DB.Notify`), as `<project uuid>:<seq>` (`realtime.Payload`), at most 56 bytes, and logs one that it cannot send. A notification wakes each subscription of its project that had not heard of the seq, and the subscription reads, as its socket's actor, in a transaction of the project's workspace, once it has checked there that the actor reads the project (`project.Service.GetIn`), the operations after the last that it was sent (`commands.ReadLog`): a page of at most 100, the first whatever its size and each of the next seq after it while those before it take less than a mebibyte as JSON, however small the log keeps them, compressed, which the page reads one operation at a time, so that what a delivery holds in memory is bounded. A delivery sends what the project had when it read its first page, and leaves what commits meanwhile for the next, so that a project that commits faster than its client reads keeps nothing else of the socket waiting, and the credential is checked again before each of its pages. At most 2 of a process's checks of a credential and reads of the log run at once, so that a busy project's many subscribers do not take the pool from the requests, and a socket reads no more of its client's messages while 32 wait, rather than hold more of them; since the library reads a pong after the messages before it, a ping that times out while the socket waits, or once it has read a message since, closes nothing. Once the hub `LISTEN`s again, and every 30 seconds, it reads the seq of each project that its sockets subscribe to (`commands.ProjectSeqs`, a statement for each workspace), and wakes the subscriptions of those whose seq it had not heard of, as a subscription that reads a seq that the hub had not heard of wakes the others of its project, so that a notification lost while it reconnected, or never sent, delays an operation, and never drops it; a project that its workspace no longer has wakes them too, and they find it gone. **Messages:** JSON, one to a text message, whose `type` names it, as `/schema/realtime/v1/messages.schema.json` describes them, which the package's types generate (`realtime.Schema`, `TestSchemaFile`); a server's message takes properties that its schema does not name, which a client ignores, and a client's does not, and is refused with `error` `invalid_message` past them, as a message that is not a JSON object of exactly its type's fields is; one over 4 KiB closes the socket with 1009. A client sends `subscribe` (`project_id`, `since`) and `unsubscribe` (`project_id`). A subscription is answered with `subscribed` and the project's seq, when `since` is missing, which starts it from that seq, or when the log holds the operation after `since` and at most 10,000 follow it (`realtime.MaxCatchUp`), which are sent first; else with `resync_required` and the project's seq, which it goes on from: the log no longer holds what follows `since`, too much follows it, or `since` is beyond the project's seq, as of a database restored from a backup older than the client. A gap that a page finds later, as compaction dropping what a subscription had not been sent, is `resync_required` too, with the seq that it goes on from, and so is a subscription of which more than 10,000 wait to be sent at once, as once its hub missed their notifications. Each operation is `operation` (`project_id`, `seq`, `commands`, each `command_id`, `type`, and `payload` as the pipeline validated it, `revisions`, each `id`, as `nd_…`, and `rev` after the batch, and `created_at`), without the inverse, which only the batch's answer holds, and without who applied it. A subscription is refused with `subscription_failed`, whose code is `invalid_id`, `not_found` for a project that does not exist and one that the actor cannot read alike, as one in the trash, one of a workspace that the actor is no member of, one not granted to a guest, or one other than the one that a token is narrowed to, `rate_limited` with `retry_after`, or `too_many_subscriptions`, past 100 of the socket (`realtime.MaxSubscriptions`); a subscription to a project that the socket subscribes to already starts anew once it holds, and one that is refused leaves the one before as it was, but for `not_found`, which ends it. The server's messages name a project by its ID as the API writes it, whichever spelling of it `subscribe` had (`ids.Decode`), but for `invalid_id`. `unsubscribe` is answered with `unsubscribed` `requested`, and a subscription whose project the actor can no longer read, as once their membership is removed or the project goes to the trash, ends with `unsubscribed` `not_found`, at its project's next operation. The server sends `heartbeat` every 25 seconds, beside a ping, whose pong it waits 20 seconds for, past which it closes the socket with 1008, unless it has read a message of the client meanwhile, or waits to. **Credentials:** the route is of `api.ReadProject`, takes a token with `project:read`, and counts in the connect bucket (`api.BucketConnect`, NFR-SEC-06), 60 a minute for an IP address and 30 for a user or a token. The socket's credential is checked again as a request's is, the session (`account.Service.Session`), whose use it records as a request's, or the token, with `project:read` (`apitoken.Service.Authenticate`), whose use counts in the workspace's API requests as a request's, before each subscription and before each page of operations, and a socket whose credential no longer holds closes with 4001 (`realtime.CloseUnauthenticated`), so that a revoked session is sent nothing more (FR-AUTH-02 AC1); each subscription counts in the read bucket. A user has at most 50 sockets open to a process (`realtime.MaxConnectionsPerUser`), past which the upgrade is answered with 429 `rate_limited`, `details.reason` `connections`, and `Retry-After`. A socket of the session cookie whose `Origin` names neither the public URL's origin nor the request's host is refused with 403 `csrf_failed`, since a browser sends the cookie with a WebSocket of any page, which `refuseCrossSite` lets through as a GET; a socket of a token is not, since no page sends an `Authorization` header with one. A request that does not upgrade, as one without `Upgrade: websocket`, a `Sec-WebSocket-Key` of 16 bytes, or `Sec-WebSocket-Version: 13`, is answered with 426 `upgrade_required`, a new code, with `Upgrade` and `Sec-WebSocket-Version`. The route walks of FR-API-01 AC1 and FR-AUTH-04 take its 426 for reached (`upgradeRoutes`), since a recorder cannot be upgraded. **Lifetime:** the server's shutdown closes the sockets with 1001, once a write in flight has finished, within 5 seconds, past which it is cut, which `http.Server.Shutdown` does not wait for, and a socket that cannot read the database closes with 1011; a client connects again after either, with backoff, and subscribes with the seqs that it has. **Metrics:** `storycreep_websocket_connections` and `storycreep_websocket_subscriptions`; the broadcast lag of NFR-OBS-02 lands in item 115. **Benchmark:** `BenchmarkIdleConnections` opens 1,000 sockets by hand, each subscribed to the `large` fixture's project, and reports the growth of the heap and the stacks in use as `MB-per-1000-sockets`, against NFR-PERF-17's 250 MB, which FR-RT-01 AC4 holds a process to. | 52, 56 |
